# SF-E2: In what framework should states govern AI, and where is a common floor?

Area E · As of clef 7924 Berichte · Luna 7924 Berichte · d3-ultra 4771 Berichte · JEV 5667 Berichte · amos 7924 Berichte · SubQ 7924 Berichte · drex 7924 Berichte

## Subquestions

- [SF-E2-T01](../../fragen/SF-E2/T01/): If the UN Security Council is not the right forum for AI security, which forum would each state accept as legitimate, who decides its members, and what exactly would the Council keep: the power to act, or the power only to be told?
- [SF-E2-T02](../../fragen/SF-E2/T02/): A lab proposes developing international frontier standards through a network of named AI safety institutes; a state calls for a global framework with broad consensus. What role would each side have in setting the agenda and approving the standards before they are treated as shared?
- [SF-E2-T03](../../fragen/SF-E2/T03/): Can two rival delegations jointly write down which concrete institutional features both would consider illegitimate, so that a common floor becomes visible even where agreement does not yet exist?
- [SF-E2-T04](../../fragen/SF-E2/T04/): Will the delegations involved state on the record whether their current talks aim only at an exchange-and-notification regime or also at shared agenda-setting, and which domestic doctrine excludes the option not chosen?
- [SF-E2-T05](../../fragen/SF-E2/T05/): Which governance framework of its own does a state propose for AI beyond the risks and measures it has already named, and how does it differ from the labs' proposals? This was asked about China: Minister Chen Yixin's article, compared with OpenAI's standards proposal and the four levels of possible agreement in the essay “We Must Pace the Frontier” by Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei.
- [SF-E2-T06](../../fragen/SF-E2/T06/): Has a government answered a lab's proposal directly? This was asked about China and the essay “We Must Pace the Frontier” by Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei; Minister Chen Yixin's article appeared one day later, a direct answer is not documented.
- [SF-E2-T07](../../fragen/SF-E2/T07/): Would governments support a jointly funded, open international research programme on problems of aligning AI with intended goals and values (deception detection, value learning, scalable oversight), with shared benchmarks and publication, modelled on the European Organization for Nuclear Research (CERN) or the International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA)?
- [SF-E2-T08](../../fragen/SF-E2/T08/): Can two states discuss “political security” and “social governance” AI risks in the same technical channel as cyber and military risks without either side treating the other's political system as the problem to be solved?

## Reports by share of levels 4–7

Levels 4–7 / 3–7 = probability each model gives to these levels (3 = useful background or context, 4 = contains some facts that could be cited as evidence … 7 = directly and substantially answers the question). Listed: reports where clef or Luna give levels 3–7 more than 50 %. Sorted by the higher of clef and Luna for levels 4–7. Further models (JEV, amos, d3 …) rated part of the reports; the others show “not yet rated”. The full distribution over the 8 levels is in the data file.
